# Evidence log

The running record of evidence (news, feedback, primary documents) as it arrives. Append a row each time something lands; tie it to the requirement(s) it affects, give it a trust tier, and note the action taken (revise / validate / no-change). The daily baseline freezes this file, so the evolution of the evidence base is visible across days.

**ID convention:** `E-YYYY-MM-DD-NN` (NN = sequence within the day).
**Trust tiers:** A = primary/authoritative · B = vendor-primary (true but promotional) · C = specialist trade press · D = secondary/aggregator. (See ADR-001 source-trust assessment.)
**Action:** `revise` (changed a decision/status) · `validate` (confirmed an existing one) · `watch` (noted, no change yet).

Confirms the as-is fail-soft reality (radio loss → stop at next station) the FRMCS design must improve on (R4) and the GSM-R operating baseline the cutover must preserve (R2). NB: confirms the STRUCTURAL fallback gap, NOT the specific "backup also failed on 23 Jun" event (stays E-16/C-tier). Copyrighted DB doc — paraphrased only |

| E-2026-06-24-21 | 2026-06-24 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (117) 5/2025, "Mobilfunk im Digitalen Knoten Stuttgart" (Bhadran, Dang, Eckhardt, Raichle, Reinhart, Thude — DB Projekt Stuttgart-Ulm / DB InfraGO / DB Regio / Verkehrsministerium BW) — first-hand practitioner account of Germany's flagship FRMCS pilot (DKS): ETCS L2 "ohne Signale" (L2oS), ATO GoA2, ~500 route-km, ~491 trains, ~1,700/day from end-2026. Paraphrased: GSM-R runs the high-load core to 2026 with special resilience, FRMCS for the surrounding area from ~2030; "aus einem Guss" coordinated equipping of GSM-R+FRMCS+public mobile; vehicle retrofit (Alstom, mid-2021) two-stage (stage 1 antennas/cables/netboxes now; stage 2 modems/filters + SW update early 2030s) at a few €k/EMU; Federal funding of 333 regional EMUs tied to 24 technical conditions; FRMCS ordered for 130 Alstom Coradia Max + 28 Siemens Mireo; RESILIENCE (§3.1): 99.992% BTS availability for ETCS L2oS via 3-BTS loops; 19 BTS failures among ~40 BTS 2018–20 + 2 BSC failures, effects mostly minor (trains pass dead spots, no auto-brake on L2oS, 40 s recovery); a hot-standby dual-layer was CONSIDERED and REJECTED (≈2× cost, detect-fail-then-restart with ops suspended, shared-frequency burden); instead condensed/overlapping BTS spacing + alternating BTS–BSC loop links so a BTS/chain/BSC loss is ridden through within the 40 s timer, doubled min level −92 dBm, tunnel-BTS batteries, prioritised maintenance; SPECTRUM: extended GSM-R 2×3 MHz (15 carriers) below the interoperable base band (2×4 MHz, 19 carriers), FRMCS at 1900 MHz, worst-case spec to meet TSI CCS (~2027), FRMCS planning on MORANE2 basis from ~2026/27; TRANSITION: 2030–2035 GSM-R (900 MHz) as temporary FRMCS fallback, then after switch-off its 900 MHz band reused to ~double FRMCS coverage; candid Hemmschuh = shortage of qualified rail-specific planners/approvers, EBA approval ≥1 yr | Technical journal (operator-authored, primary) | SIGNAL+DRAHT (DVV Media Group), 5/2025 — authored by the DKS project team (DB / BW Ministry); paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R1, R2, R3, R4, R5, R7, R13 | validate | Tier A/C: first-hand operator/practitioner account of DB's own live FRMCS pilot (primary, A) carried in a technical journal with mild project-advocacy framing (C); not vendor PR. CONFIRMS R2 (phased dual-network parallel run; hybrid vehicles — one antenna set for GSM-R/FRMCS/public mobile) and R1 timeline (core GSM-R to 2026; FRMCS from ~2030; 2030–35 dual-run; switch-off then 900 MHz reuse). R3/R4: primary evidence of DB's radio-ACCESS resilience (loop redundancy + overlapping coverage so no single BTS/BSC stops ops; real degraded-mode behaviour) — but ACCESS-LAYER, node-scoped; it does NOT address the national-core SPOF the 23 Jun 2026 outage exposed (E-15/-18), and DB explicitly chose AGAINST a hot-standby dual-layer → R3 stays open, R4 stays the PoC objective (informs, not closes). Substantiates R13 (funded 333-EMU retrofit; cheap two-stage retrofit; personnel/approval bottleneck = the feasibility risk). Corroborates R5 (ATO GoA2/ETCS L2oS; ATO data via public mobile until FRMCS) and R7 (extended GSM-R band, 1900 MHz, TSI CCS, MORANE2). No status change on any affected req → no ADR/matrix-status edit; refs only. ADR-001 §1.7 coexistence/fallback corroborated (GSM-R-as-fallback; public-mobile ≠ safety substitute) |

| E-2026-06-27-01 | 2026-06-27 | ZDFheute (ZDF), 26 Jun 2026 15:06 — DB-CONFIRMED cause of the 23–24 Jun GSM-R outage. Paraphrased: a PLANNED swap of a network distribution component (Netzwerkverteilkomponente) in the GSM-R system triggered a SOFTWARE ERROR; the fault raised NO automatic alarm, so the system did NOT automatically fail over to its redundant backup; redundancy existed but auto-switchover did not engage → nationwide loss until MANUAL recovery (~00:30, ~2 h); cyberattack ruled out. Interim DB measures: further component swaps suspended pending a manufacturer fix; maintenance windows restricted to 00:00–04:00. GSM-R stays in service; FRMCS ~10 yrs out. Quotes DB press statements + Philipp Nagl (DB InfraGO) | News (public broadcaster) + DB official confirmation | zdfheute.de (ZDF) reporting DB press statements + Nagl, 26 Jun 2026 | A/C | R3, R4, R12 | revise | A/C — DB-OFFICIAL confirmation (named CEO + DB statements, confirmed not preliminary) carried by ZDF (national public broadcaster). CLOSES the cascade-mechanism thread: network-distribution-component swap → silent software fault → automatic failover to existing redundancy did NOT trigger (no alarm) → nationwide loss until manual recovery. Confirms E-15 (swap) + the E-2026-06-25-01 software-fault lead; REFINES E-16 (backup didn't fail — auto-failover didn't engage). KEY R3 lesson: the SPOF was a DETECTION/failover gap, not absent redundancy → geo-redundancy necessary but insufficient; failover must be health-/detection-driven. R4: full standstill + manual recovery confirms the fail-soft gap. R12: a SILENT central fault is exactly the awareness gap the Risk Sentinel targets — strong validation. SUPERSEDES the HLR/VLR hypothesis (annex/PR11): confirmed element is a network distribution component, NOT the HLR/VLR. Consistent with E-2026-06-25-02 §2.2.5 (silent-fault/bad-deploy mode). Residual: no primary DB release/EBA report read directly — verify if published. No status-letter change → matrix refs + annex revised, PR11 reframed, PR5 validated |

| E-2026-06-28-06 | 2026-06-28 | CSM-RA significant-change framework VERIFIED vs primary law (EUR-Lex) — Commission Implementing Reg (EU) No 402/2013 (Common Safety Method for risk evaluation & assessment), under the Railway Safety Directive (2004/49/EC Art 6(4); now recast as Dir (EU) 2016/798). Confirms the open-letter framing (E-2026-06-28-05) is ACCURATE: Art 4(2)(a)–(f) sets exactly six significance criteria — (a) failure consequence (credible worst case), (b) novelty, (c) complexity, (d) monitoring/Überwachbarkeit, (e) reversibility, (f) additionality (cumulative recent changes); the PROPOSER decides significance by expert judgement (Art 4). If NOT significant → "keeping adequate documentation to justify the decision shall be sufficient" (Art 2(2)(b)), no independent assessment. If SIGNIFICANT → full CSM risk-management process (Art 5, Annex I) + MANDATORY independent assessment by an assessment body (AsBo) (Art 6) | Regulatory (primary law) | EUR-Lex CELEX:32013R0402 (Reg (EU) 402/2013), Arts 2(2)(b), 4, 5, 6, Annex I | A | R3, R4 | revise | A-tier primary-law verification (cf. ADR-003/E-07 for the AI Act). GROUNDS the open letter's central thesis (E-2026-06-28-05) and PR12/ADR-011: the six significance criteria + the significant→AsBo path are accurate to Art 4/Art 6; the swap's significance is the proposer's (DB's) documented expert judgement. RESIDUAL (preserved): whether DB classified the 23 Jun swap as significant vs routine is NOT public → NO regulatory breach asserted; framing sound, answer unknown. NB: enabling Dir 2004/49/EC now recast as Dir (EU) 2016/798; amendment Reg (EU) 2015/1136 not confirmed verbatim in this fetch. Separate verifies still open: EN 50128 (safety-SW change) + VO (EU) 2018/762 (SMS competence). No status-letter change → grounds PR12/ADR-011; closes the CSM-RA open-thread |

REFINES/CORRECTS the hypothesis: DB GSM-R is NOT "Nokia prime / Kontron sub" — it is RAN-by-region (Nokia south; Siemens CC + Huawei north) + core (Kapsch→Kontron). Nokia is a genuine DB GSM-R supplier but RAN/south only, standalone. Confirms Kapsch→Kontron (acquisition) — partially closes the Nortel-lineage open-thread (Nortel ≈58%/insolvency-year still unsourced). R8 nuance: legacy GSM-R = multi-vendor regional (incl. E-2026-06-24-04; E-2026-06-29-01 Nokia-centric lab). RESIDUAL: trade-press, not primary — verify vs DB/TED award records. No status change (R8 stays Recommended — policy-level open) |

| E-2026-06-30-03 | 2026-06-30 | ITU-T Recommendation Q.752 "Monitoring and measurements for Signalling System No. 7 networks" (first 1988-11; rev 1997-06) — verified vs primary (ITU-T): specifies monitoring/measurement of SS7 signalling (MTP, SCCP, ISUP, TCAP) — monitoring SIGNALLING-TRAFFIC anomalies/performance/utilisation/availability, distinct from device hardware alarms. Basis for the long-standing telecom pattern of INDEPENDENT, passive, out-of-band signalling-PROBE networks (NetScout/Tektronix, SPAN/mirror taps) that detect faults a network element does NOT self-report ("components can lie about their health") | Standard (primary) + industry pattern | ITU-T Rec Q.752 (itu.int) — primary standard; passive-probe architecture = industry practice (engagement input) | A | R3, R4, R12 | revise | A-tier primary standard (ITU-T), verified. GROUNDS the detection control for the 23 Jun failure mode (E-2026-06-27-03: silent fault, no alarm → health-based failover didn't trigger): independent out-of-band signalling monitoring detects the fault the element does NOT report. SHARPENS PR11 — "health-driven failover" is insufficient if it trusts the element's self-report; the trigger must be INDEPENDENT (probe-based). Strengthens PR5 (detection gap), R12 (the agentic Risk Sentinel can ride the probe feed), ADR-007 (detection-driven failover surface) and ADR-011 (detection precondition). Cause-AGNOSTIC: catches a silent fault whether from a bad software load (confirmed cause) or a hypothetical compromise — and serves threat detection too (ADR-001 §1.8). NB the 23 Jun outage was NOT a cyberattack (E-2026-06-27-03). Q.752 is legacy-SS7 era; the FRMCS analogue is out-of-band 5G SBA/observability monitoring — verify the modern equivalent at design |

Restates the confirmed mechanism: software fault during a planned network-component (Netzwerkverteilkomponente / "switch") swap → NO alert / no error logging → automatic failover to functional redundancy did not activate → ~90 min manual switchover. | E-2026-07-01-04 | 2026-07-01 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (114) 03/2022, "5G für das digitalisierte Bahnsystem der Zukunft — ein Ausblick auf FRMCS" (Marsch, Fritzsche, Holfeld, Kuo — ALL DB Netz AG / Digitale Schiene Deutschland) — the DSD team's FOUNDATIONAL FRMCS overview + roadmap (predates the Erzgebirge field article E-2026-07-01-03). Paraphrased: digital-rail drivers (ETCS L3 Moving Block, ATO GoA2→GoA4, sensor/video, >3.5 Mbps/train, ~10 ms E2E URLLC; EU +10 MHz @1900 MHz per Impl Dec 2021/1730 + 2×5.6 MHz @900). FRMCS design principles: (1) DECOUPLE connectivity from rail applications — different lifecycles, and explicitly to avoid lengthy re-homologation on changes for safety-relevant apps; (2) max COTS. Transport stratum (5G core/NR + non-3GPP Wi-Fi/satellite) + service stratum (MCX); FRMCS Onboard Gateway maps apps→radios; railways own their FRMCS infra (mandatory in DE for safety apps) + public network as backup/capacity. DB ROADMAP: build own FRMCS from ~2025 → nationwide GSM-R→FRMCS transition ~2035; ATO GoA2 initially on PUBLIC networks (not safety-critical then) → ported to DB FRMCS ~2027; GoA4 over DB FRMCS ~2030+. Standardisation UIC/ETSI/3GPP/ECC; ERA TSI 2022 = first FRMCS version. R&D: Ericsson (5G core/QoS), Kontron (MCX), Nokia (resilience + redundancy RAN+Core+fibre), Vodafone (public fallback for non-safety data). Field: Hamburg S-Bahn GoA4 shunting over 5G (Siemens/Nokia, AVVO, ITS 2021); Erzgebirge testbed; 5GRail | Technical journal (operator-authored; primary) | SIGNAL+DRAHT 03/2022 (DVV Media) — authored entirely by DB Netz / DSD; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R1, R2, R4, R5, R6, R7, R8 | validate | A/C: first-hand DB Netz/DSD operator account (all authors DSD — no vendor co-authors) in a technical journal; forward-looking roadmap (some content = DB plans, not results). Industry-association GUIDANCE, self-described "operative unverbindliche Empfehlungen" — non-binding; explicitly not legal advice, not usable vs authorities. Paraphrased: EU Cyber Resilience Act (CRA, Reg (EU) 2024/2847) = first horizontal cybersecurity regulation for "products with digital elements" (PDE) on the EU market from 11.12.2027 (reporting/Meldepflichten from 11.09.2026); non-conformity → sanctions up to €15M or 2.5% global turnover (Art 64) + possible recall (Art 13(21)). Rail framing: long lifecycles/dev-cycles; roles (Hersteller/Händler/Einführer/Integrator/Eigentümer/Betreiber); risk-based approach (IEC 62443 risk model, ZCR zone/conduit, SL); vulnerability mgmt + security updates + SBOM/CVE; SRAC/SecRAC (Safety-/Security-Related Application Conditions, from TS 50701) passed manufacturer→user; "CRA und Zulassung" incl. Cross-refs BSI TR-03183, ERJU System Pillar, NIS-2 (Dir (EU) 2022/2555; German NIS2UmsG in force 06.12.2025). NIS-2: transposition deadline 17.10.2024 (Art 41; repealed NIS1 from 18.10.2024); Annex I "sectors of high criticality" INCLUDES Transport (rail); Art 21 = cyber risk-management measures; Art 23 = incident reporting (24 h early-warning / 72 h / 1-month final). | E-2026-07-01-09 | 2026-07-01 | ETSI TS 103 147 V1.1.1 (2013-06), "Railway Telecommunications (RT); GSM-R Core Network Redundancy" — ETSI Technical Specification (TC-RT; ref DTS/RT-0026). PRIMARY normative standard for GSM-R core-network redundancy. Paraphrased: GSM-R is safety- and QoS-critical, so CN redundancy is needed to cope with MSC single outages — explicitly incl. PLANNED events "e.g. maintenance activities" that cause MSC/core downtime. Redundancy = combination of three 3GPP features: RANflex (Intra-domain RAN→multiple CN nodes, "MSC pool", Rel-5, TS 123 236) for p2p; Coexistence of VGCS/VBS + RANflex (Rel-7); GCSMSC + GCR Redundancy for VGCS/VBS (Rel-11, backup Group-Call-Serving-MSC + synchronized Group Call Register). Mostly 'basic' 1+1 (≤2 MSCs), also 1+N. KEY requirement (§4.2): "redundancy without the need of physical intervention in case of disaster recovery, i.e. AUTOMATIC switchover, NO manual switchover"; backup MSC has same info as failed one; not required to maintain ongoing calls (calls drop, service restores) | Standard (primary — ETSI TS) | ETSI TS 103 147 V1.1.1 (2013); paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A | R3, R4 (+ R7, PR11, incident-annex) | validate | A-tier PRIMARY normative standard — the ETSI spec for the exact thing that failed on 23 Jun: GSM-R CORE-NETWORK REDUNDANCY. ANCHORS R3 (eliminate central SPOF: MSC pool / 1+1 / 1+N, GCR redundancy) and R4 (fail-soft) in a standards requirement, not assertion. DECISIVE for the incident: §4.2 REQUIRES "automatic switchover, no manual switchover" — the 23-Jun outage (auto-failover did not engage → ~90 min MANUAL recovery, E-2026-06-27-03) is a NON-CONFORMANCE to this ETSI requirement; and §4.1 explicitly names "maintenance activities" as a case redundancy must cover — exactly the 23-Jun trigger. Sharpens PR11 (silent-fault defeats auto-failover) with a normative yardstick. NB era: GSM-R/Rel-11 (2013) — applies to the legacy + bridge estate in service now; the SAME automatic/health-driven-failover requirement carries to the FRMCS 5GC/IMS target. No status change → matrix refs R3/R4; note incident-annex + PR11 |

KEY resilience principles ("at least DUAL redundancy on ALL levels"): (1) GEO-REDUNDANT core + OSS + MCX app servers in ACTIVE-ACTIVE (2 core DCs, disaster-recovery); (2) DUAL radio coverage, two layers (interleaved / 1900-split / 900-overlay), each linked to BOTH core sites; (3) REDUNDANT multi-path IP backbone. Resiliency vs failure types: radio-head failure (UE re-attaches / dual connectivity to 2 RUs), train-UE failure (multiple UEs, redundancy masked at application level); reliability graded by #UEs × #spectrum (best: double-UE-double-spectrum survives RU + UE loss). Spectrum: ECC Decision 20(02) Nov 2020 (2×5.6 MHz@900 FDD + 10 MHz@1900 TDD RMR) + EC implementing decision Sep 2021; 1900 = first FRMCS band, 900 = base/overlay, 900 GSM-R+FRMCS coexistence "under discussion". Three onboard options: (Arch 1) single-radio FALLBACK, one IM SIM, DB↔MNO interconnected via N32 (QoS/control) + N9 (user plane) — compensates IM RADIO-ACCESS failures + poor connectivity, likely in early FRMCS spec, BUT CANNOT compensate CORE-network / service-server failures (multi-minute interruption without IM-network optimisation); (Arch 2A) dual-radio FAILSAFE, IM SIM + MNO SIM, not interconnected, public interfaces the DB FRMCS service server via N33 (QoS) + N6 (data), needs advanced multipath — compensates IM radio-access + 5G-CORE failures, but requires N33-for-MCX spec + public-MNO support; (Arch 2B) dual-radio FAILSAFE, two IM SIMs (one roaming to MNO), N32+N9 — all specs available BUT again CANNOT compensate core/service-server failures. CONCLUSION: fallback (Arch 1) suitable for FIRST FRMCS rollouts; failsafe adds reliability + data-rate but higher onboard complexity; Arch 2A ultimately RECOMMENDED (higher reliability) — but N33 for Mission Critical Services must be concluded in 3GPP; full parallel use needs future multipath study | Technical study exec-summary (operator + vendor collaboration) | Vodafone + DB Netze/DSD Executive Summary, ~2021; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R4 (+ R2, R3, R8, PR15) | validate | A/C: joint OPERATOR (DB Netze/DSD) + VENDOR (Vodafone) hybrid-architecture study — the Vodafone study named on the DSD landing page (E-2026-07-01-11); a fairly neutral options analysis (advantages/disadvantages + open points), Vodafone framing vendor-weighted (B), DB/DSD-grounded analysis + candid limits A-weight. Investigates the SIL4 Data Center: a modular, COTS-based, virtualised, GEO-REDUNDANT "Safe Computing Platform" for safety-critical TRACKSIDE apps up to SIL-4 (Advanced Protection System APS = ETCS/RBC evolution; digital interlockings; ATO; incidence prevention). Paraphrased: COTS HW + virtualization + RTE (safety layer/runtime) + applications; SAFETY via parallel synchronous cyclic application REPLICAS (2oo3, 2x2oo2); separation of safety/non-safety parts; safe monotonic clock; SRACS. GEOGRAPHICAL REDUNDANCY (§4.11): geo-red replicas across ≥2 sites, instant switchover, residual availability, SPLIT-BRAIN handling. Security architecture (§4.12): TIME/LOG/SIEM/PKI/IAM/software-update/IDS per X2Rail-3. Migration concept for legacy → RCA-compliant. CANDID UNSOLVED CHALLENGES (§8): modular multi-vendor integration + homologation complexity rises DRAMATICALLY vs EULYNX; recertification on system changes; overall integration/homologation RESPONSIBILITY + juridical recording of cross-vendor influences "still to be defined / unsolved"; cross-vendor integration of safety-relevant parts "unsolved in today's setups"; and — KEY — cloud-like DYNAMIC RESOURCE MANAGEMENT "directly affect the safety case and cannot easily be fulfilled by non-safety-relevant software for cloud infrastructures" | Research report (operator + vendor collaboration) | DB × Siemens Mobility "SIL4 Data Center" research report V1.0, 2021-10-06; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R11 (+ R3, R4, R8, R14, ADR-004/011/012, PR12/PR13/PR16) | validate | A/C: joint OPERATOR (DB/DSD) + VENDOR (Siemens Mobility) research report — A-weight for the DB/DSD-grounded requirements + RCA/OCORA/EULYNX/EN-5012x standards analysis + CANDID unsolved-challenges (anti-promotional); Siemens SIL4-DC design framing vendor-weighted (B); NB Siemens = signalling/interlocking incumbent (interest angle). HEADLINE — FIRST substantial EXTERNAL grounding for R11 / ADR-004 (until now NO logged external evidence): RCA/OCORA + DB/Siemens "Safe Computing Platform" = the industry reference for ADR-004's certified SIL-4 vital domain (RTE + replicas + safety/non-safety separation + EN-5012x homologation). Independently SUPPORTS ADR-004 Option-A (segregation) over Option-B (shared/cloud partitioning): cloud dynamic-resource-mgmt "directly affects the safety case" + mixed-criticality complexity rises dramatically → the Option-B case is as fragile as assessed. R3/R4 (compute side): geo-red REPLICAS (2oo3/2x2oo2), instant switchover + SPLIT-BRAIN = the safety-critical-COMPUTE resilience pattern (distinct layer from FRMCS-bearer resilience E-2026-07-01-12). R8: RCA/OCORA VENDOR-INDEPENDENCE / open multi-vendor platform = industry anti-concentration answer — BUT candid warning that multi-vendor integration/homologation is dramatically more complex + "unsolved" (tempers R8's unbundling recommendation). Method: OR + AI — timed EVENT GRAPHS (nodes = train-run events; edges = min run/dwell times + speed-dependent MIN HEADWAYS encoding the MOVING-BLOCK safety logic of the DSD Zielbild); a DRL agent (GNN backbone) selects intervention sequences (re-route, re-order at infrastructure elements) minimising total delay; MIP propagates changes; INVALID solutions repaired/ROLLED BACK; interfaces to LeiDis. EVALUATION (OFFLINE, test scenarios): real Magdeburg Hbf section (29 Betriebsstellen, 343 track-km), 7 scenarios, 20–35 trains/2 h: DRL always beats FIFO; Model A beats Branch-and-Cut (Gurobi, 1 h cap) in 6/7; ~10–20 s roughly CONSTANT compute vs B&C 3609 s at 35 trains; generalisation tested on unseen scenarios (Model B, mostly B&C-comparable). Training 43–73 h (11 CPU + T4 GPU). CANDID maturity: "evolutionary CTMS product PROTOTYPE"; DRL "may only approximate" the optimum; product-readiness needs richer constraints/business rules, extended dispositions (cancellation, RU coordination), refined criteria/metrics | Technical journal (operator-authored + AI-partner) | SIGNAL+DRAHT 1+2/2024 (DVV Media); DB Netz + InstaDeep; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R9 (+ R10, R11, ADR-004, ADR-010, PR6) | validate | A/C: first-hand OPERATOR account (DB Netz, 6 authors) of its OWN AI system in a technical journal; InstaDeep (the DRL builder) co-authorship = mild vendor-weighting, but a methods paper, not marketing. PATTERN PRECEDENT for ADR-004/R10/R11: the learning agent chooses ONLY within a deterministically-CONSTRAINED envelope (safety = min-headway/moving-block constraints IN the event graph; invalid interventions rolled back; dispositions execute through the existing vital layer) — an in-domain instance of "AI decides operationally, safety enforced elsewhere"; decision class = reversible/operational (duty-manager oversight per the decision-class model), NOT safety-critical actuation. ADR-010/PR6: its eval methodology (memorise-vs-generalise Model A/B, unseen scenarios, B&C/FIFO baselines, quality-vs-compute trade) is directly the shape of the agent-eval harness. No status change → matrix ref R9 |

KEY CANDID STATEMENT: any eventual deployment of AI models in trains requires homologation — "a process that is CURRENTLY LACKING A DEFINED PATHWAY"; a CERTIFIED TOOLCHAIN + substantial test data are believed to be the vital components for it. (2) SCI-CC_LST: 100% STANDARDISED interface between interlocking and operating levels, developed JOINTLY with all manufacturers → manufacturer/design independence, ESTW + DSTW (and legacy) operated identically, "opens competition"; interface RECORDS DATA STREAMS → significantly better fault analyses. (3) OPERATIONAL TRIAL candour: "no laboratory situation can replace actual operations" — despite full manufacturer ITU + acceptance testing, previously UNDETECTED errors appeared only under live heavy operation; 9-month structured trial, weekly ops/maintenance/dev triage, anomaly workflow, Wireshark pre-analysis of recorded protocols, scripted human-readable translation → FAULT SOURCES CORRECTLY ATTRIBUTED (previously blamed on the operating system; e.g. fault-message flood traced to overhead-line interference + cable fault). (4) HUMAN FACTORS: heuristic evaluation + ISO 9241 questionnaires + DLR-based stress/workload SELF-ASSESSMENT of dispatchers in highly automated human-machine systems. R8 HEADLINE: SCI-CC_LST = in-domain, IN-OPERATION proof that a 100% standardised interface + multi-vendor unbundling WORKS (4 vendors interoperating since 09/2023, competition explicitly opened) — direct support for R8's unbundling recommendation and a working counterpoint (at the operating level) to E-2026-07-02-01's "multi-vendor integration unsolved" warning → wired to R8 refs. R11/ADR-004: FIRST in-operation (not study) precedent for the boundary pattern — AnSi/EiSi shifts safety OUT of a layer, making it non-SIL/COTS with approval relief while the VITAL layer enforces safety on every command; honest bound: a DETERMINISTIC HMI layer carrying HUMAN-originated commands — validates the human-command-path + vital-enforcement design, NOT agent co-hosting (agents' no-command-path rule untouched). ADR-007: lab-tests-insufficient + structured trial + interface data-stream recording/independent analysis as test/observability surfaces; cause-ATTRIBUTION gain echoes PR5/R12 (23-Jun theme: faults misattributed until independently observed). R10/PR4: DLR workload self-assessment = methodological precedent for oversight-effectiveness/automation-bias measurement (ADR-010). ADR-011: release-change-during-operations via m:n flexibility. No status change → matrix ref R8 |
| E-2026-07-02-10 | 2026-07-02 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (116) 9/2024, "5G-RACOM — Feldstudie zu hybriden FRMCS-Netzen für ein resilientes Bahnfunksystem" (Karabinos — Bayerische Funknetz for DB InfraGO; Holfeld — Lead FRMCS, Fritzsche — Lead Connectivity Platforms, both DSD/DB InfraGO; Alte — Funkwerk; Taferner — Kontron Transportation; Basha — TU Ilmenau; Moessner — TU Chemnitz; Chkeri — SNCF Réseau) — the German-French 5G-RACOM innovation project (BMWK + MEFR, "Franco-German Ecosystem for Private 5G Networks", 3 yrs to end-2025, grant 01MJ22015): HYBRID FRMCS networks — public mobile (PMNO) complements the private FRMCS network as FALLBACK or capacity enhancement via MULTIPATH protocols (MP-TCP, MP-QUIC). Paraphrased: 6 use cases incl. UC2 FALLBACK (PMNO takes over on FRMCS failures/coverage gaps), UC3 resilience via PATH SELECTION (switch on QoS degradation), UC4 resilience via REPLICATION (duplicate packets on both paths simultaneously — capacity traded for critical-data reliability, NO failover trigger needed); architecture: 2 radio modules (private FRMCS SIM + PMNO SIM) = INDEPENDENT data paths; MPF client onboard (Funkwerk onboard GW) + MPF server trackside (Kontron standalone GW/proxy anchoring traffic to the MCx server), user-plane-only (N6); single-path alternatives (national roaming / MOCN) = long/small interruptions vs multipath SEAMLESS; UIC FRMCS SRS standardising multipath as an OPTIONAL feature (FSMPM control-protocol reference point). CANDID GAPS: no standalone multipath GW/proxy solution exists (current implementations e2e/application-level); DOUBLE-CONTROL-LOOP problem in GW solutions; MP-QUIC immature (middleboxes, encrypted inspection); control protocol CONCEPT-ONLY (implementation out of scope); field tests in Erzgebirge Digitales Testfeld run PROVISIONALLY at 3.7 GHz (n78) because target-band 900/1900 MHz radio equipment NOT YET AVAILABLE; public side = TU Chemnitz + Vodafone MPN slice (700/1800/3400 MHz). Also: 900/1900 MHz channel-sounding + FRMCS/GSM-R 900-MHz COEXISTENCE analysis. Results to feed UIC FRMCS spec process + ETSI | Technical journal (operator + academic + vendor consortium, publicly funded) | SIGNAL+DRAHT 9/2024 (DVV Media); DB InfraGO/DSD + SNCF Réseau + TU Ilmenau/Chemnitz + Funkwerk/Kontron; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R4 (+ R3 bounded, R2/R7, PR11) | validate | A/C: first-hand — the project's own DB InfraGO + SNCF Réseau leads on a publicly funded (BMWK/MEFR) project, unusually candid on gaps; C-frame — trade journal self-description + vendor co-authors (Funkwerk, Kontron — NB Kontron is one of R8's two concentration names). R4 HEADLINE: the sector project engineering EXACTLY R4's designed multi-bearer fallback path — public-5G fallback/resilience (UC2/3/4) made concrete, multipath entering the UIC SRS as a standardised (OPTIONAL) feature; upgrades R4's public-5G path from the Vodafone concept study (E-2026-07-01-13) to an ACTIVE implementation project with field trials → wired to R4 refs. Maturity HONESTY: validates the DIRECTION, not deliverability — research project, control protocol unimplemented, GW/proxy unsolved (double control loops), MP-QUIC immature, field tests in NON-TARGET spectrum. R3 BOUNDED: independent dual paths = BEARER/path-level resilience only — the MCx SERVICE layer behind the MPF remains the Ericsson-identified service-layer SPOF (E-2026-07-01-14); multipath does not touch it. PR11 angle (note only): UC4 REPLICATION is a TRIGGER-LESS resilience mode — duplicate always-on paths need NO silent-fault detection to fail over, directly interesting vs the 23-Jun failure class (complements Q.752 detection + config diversity; capacity cost). R2/R7: FRMCS/GSM-R 900-MHz coexistence + channel models support the parallel-run/spectrum context. No status change → matrix ref R4 |

| E-2026-07-02-13 | 2026-07-02 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (116) 12/2024, "Einführung von Zumutbarkeitskriterien in der Leit- und Sicherungstechnik" (Barz — Technical Lead DSD Project Portfolio, DB InfraGO; Menzel — General Manager, CERSS Kompetenzzentrum Bahnsicherungstechnik GmbH; reporting an ONGOING TU-Dresden DISSERTATION) — REASONABLENESS (human-workload) CRITERIA for signalling: the named GAP — Zumutbarkeit criteria for operating/engineering staff (train drivers Tf, dispatchers Fdl, planning engineers, inspection experts PSV) are NOT yet considered in the EU-wide CSM-RA process (Reg 402/2013/EC) nor the national/DB rulebook (AEG/EBO, Sektorleitlinie, EIGV, VV Bau-STE, Ril 125, Muster-TAst); DIN EN ISO 10075 / VDI 4006 not operationalised. | E-2026-07-02-14 | 2026-07-02 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (117) 1+2/2025, "Multi-Agenten KI-Ansatz für ein ganzheitliches Verkehrsmanagementsystem" (17 authors: Schneider, Safaei, Wülfing, Güral, Liessner + Küpper — CTMS Ambassador + Sturm — CTMS Product Manager/AI expert, all DSD/DB InfraGO; Roets, Deekshith — DB Systel; Stirbu, Ghriss, Siboni, Hustache, Lecat, Markin, Le, Bejjani — InstaDeep) — the MARL follow-on to the CTMS technical article (E-2026-07-02-05, its ref [4]): MULTI-AGENT deep-RL formulation for CONSTRUCTIVE schedule generation — one AI model steers every train individually (speed + next point-setting per step; local observation + compressed global state; deliberately simple reward) through a microscopic railway simulation, GENERATING new schedules for both planning and live re-dispatching (disruption runs start from a live-operations snapshot); per-train decisions parallelise → scalability. HEADLINE ARCHITECTURAL DESIGNATION (near-verbatim): the CTMS "is NOT being designed as a safety-critical system that must guarantee collision-free rail traffic. The responsibility for safety lies with the system that operates the interlockings — in this case the Advanced Protection System (APS)"; CTMS must INTEGRATE the safety system's constraints to produce executable plans, but safety authority sits BELOW it. EVALUATION (SIMULATED, Magdeburg section, 29 stations/343 track-km): dense single station — MARL 100% of 100 scenarios vs greedy baseline 69% (deadlocks without coordination); full section, 72 scenarios × 17–41 trains — 94% of train runs conflict-free (identical for planning and post-disruption dispatching; disruptions placed on DIFFERENT tracks than in training → GENERALISATION demonstrated); 100% ≤19 trains, slight decline at 41; compute < 30 s for 42 min simulated traffic, LINEAR in train count (vs exponential OR) → real-time-dispatching candidate. | E-2026-07-02-16 | 2026-07-02 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (117) 7+8/2025, "Vorbereitung und Optimierung der Eisenbahninfrastruktur für eine ETCS-Ausrüstung" (Bauer — Technical Expert Digital Design, Kalkreiber — Technical Lead Digital Design, Schie-Lüdke — Project/Programme Management, Brückner — System Architect ETCS; all DB InfraGO) — the "ETCS-READY" forward-preparation discipline: Arbeitshilfe 819.1343 NV (Feb 2025) bundles the measures ALL tangential trades (LST, telecoms, traffic systems, electrical energy, superstructure) must take IN ADVANCE of an ETCS-L2 retrofit, because ETCS L2 is the declared TARGET STATE for all current/future infrastructure projects (European Deployment Plan obligation; KRITIS/resilience framing). Paraphrased measures: signal-mast dimensioning for Ne 14; larger signal↔switching-section-boundary distances under ETCS vs PZB; RELEASE-SPEED-aware signal placement (0/5/10/15 km/h — wrong placement costs platform length); signal arrangement behind switches (balises barely relocatable within switches, Ril 819.1340/.1344); control-area boundaries ≥1050 m → unrestricted level change with partial ETCS, no monitoring gap. Retrofit measures proper: balises + RBC (ESTW sub-centre / DSTW technical site), adjacent-RBC changeover balises + RBC SW adaptation, ETCS-L2 HIGH-PERFORMANCE BLOCK (extra block markers/detection sections/axle counters, Ril 819.0519), DARK-SWITCHING of main signals to avoid external-signalling-vs-cab-display contradictions (interlocking SW update), level-crossing constraints (815 family; Hp switch-on distances designed for PZB need recalc; possibly full closures), integrated ETCS operating function, telecoms checks (radio coverage level + availability of radio components; RBC↔interlocking/media-gateway/neighbour-RBC interfaces). ECONOMIC argument: ignoring ETCS requirements in today's interlocking/construction measures → extensive later adaptation, EXTRA planning/inspection resources (scarce), higher costs, delay; up-front consideration is mostly blockage-time-neutral and ACCELERATES the rollout | Technical journal (operator-authored practice) | SIGNAL+DRAHT 7+8/2025 (DVV Media); DB InfraGO; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | ADR-011/PR14 pattern (+ R5 context, R13 adjacent) | watch | A/C: first-hand operator practice by DB InfraGO's digital-design/ETCS-architecture people, anchored in named dated DB directives (819.1343 NV etc.); C-frame — trade journal, operator describing its own tool, benefits asserted not measured. HONESTY: the MOST PERIPHERAL item of the 07-02 batch for this engagement — trackside ETCS enablement, not FRMCS/oversight. The ONE transferable idea — "X-READY" forward-compatibility as a GOVERNED discipline: a named operator instrument (819.1343 NV) forces every INTERVENING measure to anticipate the declared target state because later retrofit binds scarce planning/inspection resources (E-2026-07-02-13's PSV scarcity) and causes delay — exactly the discipline ADR-011 implies for the bridged GSM-R→FRMCS route and PR14 warns about inversely (reuse-inherits-obsolescence): every change to the in-service estate during the dual-run should be checked "FRMCS-READY" — don't build today what must be rebuilt tomorrow. R5 context: trackside half of dense ETCS-L2 enablement. R13-adjacent (NOT wired — R13 is vehicle retrofit, this is trackside). No status change; not wired to the matrix → watch |
| E-2026-07-02-17 | 2026-07-02 | SIGNAL+DRAHT (117) 6/2025, "Georeferenz in der LST — von der Planung bis zur Abnahme" (Bachmann — Head of Planning DSD Projects + Schröder, DB InfraGO; Wenzel, Braun — Nextrail; Schwöbel — CERSS) — the OPAL project (Optimierung von Plan- und Abnahmeleistungen; DB InfraGO + Nextrail + CERSS): GEOREFERENCED (GNSS) methods replace the MEASURING-WHEEL process for position-critical CCS elements from planning to ACCEPTANCE — the EXECUTION of what the D3iP article (E-2026-07-02-06, same lead author) promised. Paraphrased: wheel-method weaknesses (per-track walking, UNDETECTED error propagation from wrong absolute reference-point positions — locally plan-correct yet faulty, found only via costly acceptance runs; distance-dependent error; ambiguous reference points; manual documentation) → SOLL coordinates derived from digital planning (PlanPro, DB_REF2016), IST recorded with approved GNSS equipment, LIVE plan-vs-built deviation, lossless transfer back into planning tools; continuous as-planned/as-built comparison; digital as-built documentation as by-product → PROTOCOL-BASED ACCEPTANCE offer to the inspection expert REPLACING complete on-site inspection or reducing it to critical points; GNSS spot checks; balise-plate/telegram assurance replacing track telegram tests; FEWER ETCS acceptance-run test cases; maintenance benefits (BERLIN S-BAHN trial, standardised inventory platform). Tolerances: ±1 m installation (±0.4 install/±0.6 maintenance); ETCS worst case +15 cm still LH-BTSF-conformant; ATO ±10 cm leaves no slack. SAFETY SHIFT stated plainly: conventional safety rests on PEOPLE applying tools correctly — georeferenced shifts it to SAFETY OF THE TOOLS (coordinates not human-checkable against a distance tolerance) → OPAL recipe: FMEA of EVERY process step (human + tool errors, exported application conditions); only a FEW tools classified SAFETY-INFLUENCING per EN 50716 (2023 railway-SW standard succeeding EN 50128); INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION TOOL for coordinate derivation (certified or manually compared); CHECKSUMS + DUAL-CHANNEL procedures; 3-level argumentation (inventory-data assumptions → safe derivation → safe realisation). ASSESSED: independent Gutachten over the entire documentation CONFIRMS the acceptance-process description safety-suitable per §2(2) EBO; run in PARALLEL with the conventional process in several projects; specs revised with site supervisors + acceptance inspectors; guideline amendments in coordination (Ril 819/892/883 supplement) | Technical journal (operator-led + contractor + inspection body) | SIGNAL+DRAHT 6/2025 (DVV Media); DB InfraGO + Nextrail + CERSS; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R13 (+ R10/ADR-010 tool-qualification template, R12/PR10, ADR-004 note) | validate | A/C: first-hand operator-led project results INCLUDING a completed INDEPENDENT ASSESSMENT (a notch above the promise-stage siblings), anchored in named directives; C-frame — trade journal self-description, some benefits prospective; interest note (as E-2026-07-02-06/-13) — Nextrail (2 authors) + CERSS (1) are contractor/inspection-services parties in a piece arguing for reduced inspection effort. R13 HEADLINE: the approval-burden lever moves from PROMISE (D3iP, E-06) to ASSESSED INSTANCE — protocol-based acceptance independently assessed safety-suitable (§2(2) EBO), practice-trialled in parallel, quantified tolerances, guideline amendments underway → wired to R13 refs. R10/ADR-010: the TOOL-QUALIFICATION template complete — FMEA per step → minimal EN-50716 safety-influencing tool set → independent verification tool → checksums/dual-channel → independent assessment = the assurance shape the oversight layer's own tooling needs if agent outputs (Assurance agent evidence chain, R12/PR10) ever carry weight in safety-relevant workflows. STANDARDS-CURRENCY note: EN 50716 (2023) supersedes EN 50128 for railway software — relevant to ADR-004 action item 4's verify-the-cited-provisions task. No status change |

GroundTruth: reference ideally 10× more accurate (ANSI/NCSLI Z540.3:2006), GNSS/RTK candidates + a patented LiDAR-blur alternative (static-object blur ≈ total system error). FRMCS + public-mobile reception conditions protocolled along the route with 3D-map prediction. | E-2026-07-02-25 | 2026-07-02 | S+D (118) 3/2026, pp. 16–23 — "Georedundanz im Bahnsystem" (Schmidt — Sr Expert Digitalisation Railway Operations; Marsch — Head of Overall Technical System Architecture & IT Platforms; Knapp — Sr InfoSec Expert / EU-Rail Security Domain lead; ALL DB InfraGO): the operator's own GEO-REDUNDANCY DOCTRINE for the centralised CCS estate (DTZ data centres). Driver: centralisation raises vulnerability (extreme weather, DELIBERATE DESTRUCTION); BSI/KRITIS requires geo-redundancy (BSI-Gesetz §8a, KRITIS concretisation, BSI RZ siting criteria). Requirements set: exactly ONE of primary/fallback in control at any time; switchover procedures agreed + REGULARLY PRACTISED; fallback monitorable without touching primary availability; fallback usable for planned maintenance (measures on passive side first, then switch); switching at sub-site (iUZ) granularity. Options assessed vs explicit criteria: COLD STANDBY w/ complete fallback CHOSEN short/mid-term (identical components accepted WITH the primary incl. project config, then deactivated; every primary change promptly applied to fallback + regularly tested; no fresh approval in disaster; works with today's approved products — peripherals only ever see one active system); Warm/Hot needs fundamental CCS-spec changes → only with proven zero impact on primary; ZIELBILD: WARM STANDBY ON VIRTUALISATION (CCS functions on vendor-independent COTS in an on-premises cloud; Europe's-Rail-agreed separation of CCS applications up-to-SIL4 from the IT platform, implemented in CLOUD4RAIL — the E-2026-07-02-01/-03 platform family); GEOGRAPHICALLY DISTRIBUTED PRIMARY (2-of-3 voting) REJECTED — regular-operation availability drop unavoidable + EULYNX deviations, "unacceptable". KEY DESIGN STATEMENT: disaster switchover stays MANUAL even under virtualisation — "not because automatic is impossible, but because this serious decision should always be made by a human in a tried and tested procedure" (recovery target seconds-to-minutes). Candid open challenges: meshed topology + dynamic routing needed; safe isolation + controlled external activation of inactive systems; MONITORING/TESTING an isolated cold fallback unsolved (no complete E2E test without the track field); cross-site version parity/responsibility. (Ried — Sr Operational Expert, EU-Rail System Pillar Operational Harmonisation Domain, DB InfraGO; Schouwerwou — ERTMS advisor ProRail, 2015 co-proposer of the European Instructions; Lambert — Lead Engineer ERTMS Users Group): the WRITTEN-ORDER fallback layer under harmonisation. Facts: TSI OPE's European Instructions (EI 1–9, harmonised numbering + boxes) MANDATORY since 14 Dec 2025 for ETCS-guided trains; DB's DIGITAL BEFEHL in operation since Dec 2025 (post-trial, high satisfaction) — but DB/Infrabel/ÖBB/ProRail/SNCF Réseau each test NATIONAL digital solutions; authors candidly name the fragmentation and call on ERA to harmonise. System Pillar target-system analysis (expert-discussion state, paraphrased): GIVEN a reliable radio connection to the RBC, only FOUR of nine EIs remain needed (pass-ETCS-halt incl. SR, revocation, speed restriction, run-on-sight); the rest replaceable by technical means (MA shortening vs remain-at-standstill; Packet 88 vs level-crossing order; infrastructure data to the display; technically granted SR/OS profiles + DMI text). Design principles: minimise human speech communication; technical solutions take precedence for safety-relevant measures. | E-2026-07-02-27 | 2026-07-02 | S+D (118) 4/2026, pp. 14–20 — "Virtualisierte Safety-Architekturen in der LST" (Ansar + Wissmann — Platform Architects, Overall Technical System Architecture & IT Platforms, DB InfraGO; written within CLOUD4RAIL/IPCEI-CIS): the operator's own comparison of two architectures for hosting virtualised CCS functions UP TO SIL-4 on COTS hardware. ARCHITECTURE A — safety-CERTIFIED virtualisation (separation kernel; formal separation, static resources, bounded WCET, EN 50128/50716 + qualified toolchain — the E-2026-07-02-03 approach): cons = vendor lock-in, EVERY hypervisor/VM-config/driver update becomes SAFETY-RELEVANT (strict controls + regression evidence), major redesign of existing applications, multi-layer re-authorisation. ARCHITECTURE B — NON-certified COTS virtualisation (untrusted platform): no guaranteed isolation/determinism/cert artefacts; safety anchored in a certified vendor SAFETY LAYER ABOVE the virtualisation + NHA (Native Hardware Access — controls safety-relevant HW parameters, feeds them directly to the safety layer; in the safety case) + composite fail-safety and fault/error detection: pros = vendor/product diversity incl. open-source hypervisors, platform replaceable with little safety-relevant effort (REDUCED LOCK-IN), existing CCS applications (mostly already bundling safety layers) port with low effort; cons = higher application-level V&V, centralised safety environment a POTENTIAL SPOF if not redundant. DIRECTION: DB InfraGO + industry partners will PURSUE ARCHITECTURE B for existing trackside CCS (interlockings as software instances on a common on-premises virtualisation platform); ERJU assumes B sector-wide; Architecture A remains attractive for onboard hard-real-time (TCMS) + novel GoA-4 applications (lowers market-entry barrier). OPEN in Cloud4Rail: isolation, deterministic data distribution, replication/voting, COMMON-CAUSE FAILURES; full C4R docs to the EU IPCEI-C4R portal (not yet online at writing) | Technical journal (operator platform architects, first-hand) | SIGNAL+DRAHT 4/2026 (DVV Media); DB InfraGO/Cloud4Rail; paraphrased, not quoted (copyrighted) | A/C | R11/ADR-004 design-basis family (+ R8 lock-in, R3/PR11 adjacency, PR16 change-stream kin) | validate | A/C: the deciding architects' own two-sided comparison with hard cons named for both options; C-frame — trade venue, direction = pilots planned, C4R deliverables unpublished. R11 DESIGN-BASIS UPDATE (E-01 → E-03 → THIS): the Safe-Computing-Platform picture EVOLVES — where E-03 matured the certified separation kernel, the operator now directionally chooses UNTRUSTED platform + certified APPLICATION-LEVEL safety layer (+NHA) for existing trackside CCS: the vital domain manages its own mixed-criticality by explicit safety layers, NOT by trusting the platform. NB NAMING COLLISION: their "Architecture B" ≠ ADR-004 Option B — theirs concerns where the VITAL safety argument lives; NOTHING here extends to hosting LEARNING components (common-cause failures + replication/voting explicitly open) → ADR-004 Option A (segregate the agents) UNCHANGED, trade-off paragraph extended. Side threads: reduced-lock-in rationale = R8 kin; SPOF-if-not-redundant = R3/PR11 kin; Architecture A's platform-updates-are-safety-relevant = the PR16/ADR-011 security-update change-stream burden named from the platform side; completes E-2026-07-02-25 (the warm-standby Zielbild runs on this platform; same department, Cloud4Rail in both). | E-2026-07-05-08 | 2026-07-05 | ETSI TS 123 236 V17.0.0 (2022-04) = 3GPP TS 23.236 Rel-17 — "Intra-domain connection of RAN nodes to multiple Core Network nodes" (GSM/UMTS/LTE): primary definition of the pool/"flex" redundancy architecture — a RAN node (BSC/RNC) connects to MULTIPLE core-network nodes (MSC/SGSN pool) instead of exactly one, so losing a single core node no longer takes down its whole service area; introduced Rel-5, maintained through Rel-17. | E-2026-07-05-12 | 2026-07-05 | ERA-commissioned migration study (Systra), FINAL REPORT V1.0, 20/05/2016 — "Study on migration of railway radio communication system from GSM-R to other solutions" (project ERA 2015 04 1 RS, ref ERA_RS1_DLV_023, 132 pp., study window Sep 2015–May 2016; approval chain Maton/Marcus/Pushparatnam; part of ERA's planning sequence after Analysys Mason 2014, SCF 2015, IDATE 2015): TIMELINE BASELINE 2016 — ERA guidance to the study assumed draft FRMCS requirements/specs 2018–2019, possible deployment start 2022, GSM-R support until at least 2030; IMs and RUs both expected migration to begin 2025. PRECEDENT — the GSM-R migration itself took ≥7 years (medium MS) and up to 19 years (large MS) from planning to end. ECONOMICS (parameterised NPV model; lifecycle assumptions 15 yr trackside BSS / 12 yr cab radio) — Scenario 0 (no migration) the most expensive; long GSM-R/NG overlap costliest for IMs yet BETTER for RUs (dual-mode replacement at natural renewal) → MISALIGNMENT OF INCENTIVES: absent public-policy intervention RUs rationally delay past the global optimum; recommendations: keep using the model, IM–RU coordination as a Member-State matter, accurate rolling-stock upgrade registers (ECVVR; State-Aid conditionality suggested), and FINANCIAL COMPENSATION TO RUs. Also: GSM-R classed MOTS on 2G, "prohibitively expensive to operate circa 2030"; dual-mode cab+handheld radios the agreed migration mechanism; some IMs open to PPDR/MNO sharing models. | E-2026-07-06-10 | 2026-07-06 | Peleska & Schulz, "Reliability Analysis of Safety-Related Communication Architectures", SAFECOMP 2010 (Springer LNCS; Univ. Bremen AGBS), 6 pp. — peer-reviewed formal-methods paper on the EN 50159 SAFETY-LAYER / "GREY CHANNEL" architecture. (1) EN 50159-2 splits safety-related comms into a SAFETY LAYER (meets a SIL) over an untrusted grey channel (COTS transport, no safety responsibility); the safety layer must detect the SIX message-error threats (repetition, deletion, insertion, resequence, corruption, delay) via the standard defence matrix (seq-no, timestamp, timeout, src/dst ID, feedback, ID procedure, safety code). (2) SAFETY-VS-RELIABILITY: the safe reaction to a detected error is a SAFE STATE that STOPS comms until reset → safe communication REDUCES availability; recovered by adding a reliable transmission service (ARQ) in the grey channel. (3) CAUTION: a NAIVE combination of grey-channel + safety-layer fault-tolerance does NOT necessarily raise overall fault-tolerance (ARQ retransmits may deliver out-dated msgs the safety layer discards) — must be ANALYSED. (4) METHOD: model in DSL CAMoLa → per-fault "mutant" models → UPPAAL timed-automata check A□(SAFE ∧ ¬φ); reliability = P(correct or safe-mutant behaviour over operational period); avoids probabilistic model-checking (state-explosion). | E-2026-07-11-01 | 2026-07-11 | heise online (EN edition of DE original) BACKGROUND/ANALYSIS, Andreas Sebayang, 05.07.2026 — "FRMCS or LTE-R: Was the GSM-R succession missed?": argues AGAINST the "DB slept through the GSM-R succession" narrative. Reported facts (paraphrased): GSM-R ≈100 kbit/s capacity (UIC); ETCS L2 needs ~30 kbit/s (fits EDGE 59.2 kbit/s); FRMCS scenarios ~3.5 Mbit/s with latency ~1 s → <10 ms. LTE-R road-not-taken: South Korea Wonju–Gangneung (Gyeonggang line, 120 km) live 17.12.2017 (Samsung/KT press release; 3GPP Rel-13; 250 km/h; Busan Metro 04/2017); Huawei proprietary LTE-R marketed from 2019 (thin public documentation); Luxembourg CFL Kapsch GSM-R build 2015–2019 (analog off end-2019) as the timing illustration — LTE-R came too late for that cycle, and EU-wide interoperability/coordination made a single-nation LTE-R jump infeasible → Europe chose direct-to-5G FRMCS (3GPP-integrated to avoid proprietary lock-in). STANDARDISATION STATE: 3GPP frozen through Rel-19 (Rel-20 planned); FRMCS V2 presented 2024; UIC Plugtest 2025; V2.2 in large-scale testing; V3 scheduled NOVEMBER 2026 as the "first edition for national pilot projects"; UIC 2019 planning had parallel operation from ~2025 and network-wide conversion taking "a good ten years"; first FRMCS routes expected 2026. Scale: >450 railway companies / up to 21,000 locomotives to convert (FRMCS initiative). Vendors: Qualcomm X85/X105 railway modems, Ericsson partnership 2025. Precedent: LTE900 hardening of GSM-R brought years of delay partly because operators could not swap cab radios in time. | E-2026-07-11-02 | 2026-07-11 | heise online (EN edition of DE original) NEWS report, Nico Ernst, 27.06.2026 — "Railway failure: Maintenance only from 0 to 4 AM in future". Reported facts (paraphrased, sourced to the Nagl blog post + DB statements + dpa/SWR): 23-Jun cause = network-switch replacement triggered a "singular software error" that produced NO error message; the redundant GSM-R existed but auto-switchover did not engage because the mechanism DEPENDED on an error notification that never came; first failure reports ~22:20; fallback activated MANUALLY after ~90 min; some trains running again ~00:30. Countermeasures: (1) maintenance henceforth restricted to 00:00–04:00 — WITH THE RATIONALE spelled out: the window when primarily FREIGHT trains run, which can halt on open track without cascading consequences; (2) component swaps suspended pending error resolution with the switch MANUFACTURER; (3) DB InfraGO building a "fallback level via public mobile radio". Context: GSM-R expected in service ≥10 more years; FRMCS successor standard EU-unspecified → component procurement not yet possible. FACTUAL LEAD (relayed, not primary): Telstra mobile-network outage 08.07.2026 ~04:30 — cause a "software defect" in the "nodes that keep time across the mobile network" (network TIMING function), EXPLICITLY not a cyber incident; scope: mobile calls/data, REGIONAL RAIL services, payment systems, public transport, partially emergency Triple-Zero (000) calls; mostly resolved by late afternoon; ACMA investigation expected; Telstra previously paid a $3M penalty (12/2024) for a prior Triple-Zero disruption. REMAINDER = promotional continuity advice (dual paths, carrier diversity, automatic failover, CDN/DNS, offline runsheets, SCHEDULED FAILOVER TESTING; "redundancy is not buying a second version of everything") with explicit calls-to-action for the firm's own services | Vendor blog (MSP, promotional; factual lead relayed from Telstra statements/media) | compuloop.com.au/telstra-outage-business-continuity-failover-lessons/, fetched 2026-07-11 | B | R3, R4 (+ PR11 common-mode; R12 kin) | watch | B per the Latro precedent (E-2026-06-30-02): true-but-promotional, no independent evidentiary weight — outage facts are RELAYED; the A-tier follow-ups are Telstra's own statement + the ACMA findings (log if this becomes load-bearing). WHY RETAINED — the fact pattern is the 23-JUN CLASS REPLAYED ON A PUBLIC NETWORK, 15 days later, other side of the world: software defect in a SHARED CENTRAL FUNCTION (timing) → national mobile outage → RAIL SERVICES disrupted → cyberattack explicitly ruled out → regulator steps in. THREE HOOKS: (1) R4 — DB's post-incident direction is a "fallback level via PUBLIC mobile radio" (E-2026-06-27-03; 5G-RACOM grounding E-2026-07-11-05): Telstra demonstrates the fallback bearer carries the SAME failure class — bearer diversity must be judged at FAILURE-MODE level (shared core functions, config/software commonality), not bearer-brand level; grounds the existing multi-bearer caveat (E-2026-07-06-10) and PR11's common-mode concern with a live public-network instance; (2) TIMING as a shared dependency deserves attention in FRMCS design — timestamping/synchronisation services are IN MORANE-2's validation scope (E-2026-07-11-05) and 5G TDD is sync-sensitive: a timing-function defect is a candidate common-mode across BOTH the rail network and its public fallback; (3) the piece's own "scheduled failover testing" line restates ADR-007's prove-the-trigger discipline (noted, promotional venue). CAUSE-CLASS DISCIPLINE maintained: accident-class software fault, not attack (same rule as E-2026-06-27-01). PAPER-2 CONVERGENCE (added same day): wired into the paper-2 prompt as a SECOND FRAMING INSTANCE for point 9 (public-fallback common-mode warning) — STRICTLY at relay level: this source does NOT establish that Telstra's failover failed to trigger, so no "backup never asked" parallel may be claimed for Telstra until the Telstra/ACMA primary is logged (the flagged A-tier follow-up). No decision moved; no matrix/ADR change |
| E-2026-07-12-01 | 2026-07-12 | ABC News (AU national public broadcaster), 10.07.2026 — "Why V/Line was hit harder than other states by Telstra outage". ACCESS CAVEAT: abc.net.au blocks our fetcher — content obtained via search-index retrieval and corroborated by parallel coverage (The Conversation 08.07, Hasan/Univ. of Canberra: cause + 4G migration; Bendigo Advertiser 09.07: radio/control systems, testing, resumption). Facts: Telstra outage 08.07.2026 ~04:30 (cause per Telstra acting CEO Michael Ackland, via The Conversation: "time synchronisation in those nodes wasn't working as it should", cause-unknown at the time, no malicious activity) → V/Line (regional Victoria, >70,000 pax/day) suspended ALL services; still offline the next morning; resumed 09.07 ~13:02 after ARTC repair + V/Line's OWN INDEPENDENT comms testing between control and crews (Bendigo Adv.). WHY VICTORIA WAS HIT HARDEST: train radio AND control-system connectivity ride TELSTRA 4G — ARTC/VicTrack moved train-to-network comms to Telstra 4G in 2024 (The Conversation) — plus broader estate dependency (passenger info screens, ticketing, security also on Telstra; ABC). THE HEADLINE MECHANISM (ABC, attributed to V/Line CEO William Tieppo): the trains HAD a SATELLITE BACKUP and it "did not work correctly because of the nature of the Telstra failure" — the 4G network FLAPPED on/off, and the satellite system, hearing "4G is connected, you don't need me anymore", kept SWITCHING ITSELF OFF. Free travel/reimbursements announced; Victorian premier publicly critical of Telstra (per parallel coverage headlines) | News (public broadcaster) + operator-CEO-attributed statements; corroborating set: academic-expert outlet + regional press | abc.net.au/news/2026-07-10/why-vline-was-hit-harder-than-other-states-by-telstra-outage/106895726 (fetch-blocked, search-retrieved); theconversation.com 08.07.2026 (Hasan); bendigoadvertiser.com.au 09.07.2026 | A/C (ZDF-precedent split: A-weight for the NAMED operator/telco-official statements — Tieppo, Ackland; C press frame; MINUS the access caveat: the ABC page itself not read directly → VERIFY the Tieppo satellite quote against the page or a second carrier before publication use) | R4, R3 (+ PR11; ADR-007 trigger-proving; paper-2 point 9; extends E-2026-07-11-07) | validate | UPGRADES the Telstra thread from B-tier relay (E-2026-07-11-07) to attributed operator detail — and the detail is the engagement's core lesson INVERTED: 23-Jun DB = a SILENT fault (no signal at all → the failover was never told to act); 08-Jul V/Line = a FLAPPING fault (intermittently healthy signal → the fallback was told to STAND DOWN). Same root defect both times: FALLBACK-ENGAGEMENT LOGIC THAT TRUSTS THE PRIMARY'S SELF-REPORT — "the backup that was never asked" meets "the backup that was told it wasn't needed". FOUR WIRES: (1) R4 — the multi-bearer fallback design lesson becomes concrete: fallback triggering needs INDEPENDENT health assessment + flap-damping/hysteresis, and PARTIAL failure (flapping) is the harder test case than clean loss — the Peleska formal caveat (naive bearer combination ≠ reliability gain, E-2026-07-06-10) now has a live operational instance; (2) ADR-007 — prove-the-trigger discipline must inject REALISTIC fault modes incl. flapping/intermittent, not only clean-loss; V/Line's insistence on its OWN independent comms test before resumption = the prove-don't-trust discipline in practice; (3) PR11/R3 — public-bearer fallback carries its own common-mode + trigger-logic risks (extends E-2026-07-11-07's failure-mode-level diversity point); (4) 2024 migration of ARTC/VicTrack train comms onto a single public carrier = a live instance of the single-bearer concentration question R4/R8 carry for the FRMCS public-fallback direction. Paper-2 prompt updated to v1.5 (bounded use upgraded: documented instance, verify-flag intact). ACMA findings remain the A-tier follow-up. No status flip; no matrix/ADR status change |
| E-2026-07-12-02 | 2026-07-12 | UIC FRMCS System Requirements Specification (FRMCS SRS), FW-AT 7800 v1.0.0, 13.02.2023, FRMCS AT Working Group, 109 pp., ISBN 978-2-7461-3119-4, © UIC Paris 2023 — the FRMCS-side normative SRS: successor-side counterpart to EIRENE SRS 16.1.0 (E-2026-07-02-31) and THE document earlier rows cite as "UIC SRS" (E-2026-07-02-10/-21) — now on file, closing E-2026-07-02-31's do-not-conflate note. Document history: 40+ controlled iterations Oct 2019 → v1.0.0 official publication, with external-review rounds, EECT workshops, ETSI TC RT alignment, TOBA-SRS merge. Clause categories (M)/(O)/(I). WHAT v1.0.0 SPECIFIES (M-level, paraphrased): three-strata architecture (Railway Application / Service / Transport), decoupling as design principle — "future transport technologies will be introduced as FRMCS evolves with no impact on railway applications" (§12.1.1), FRMCS shall interface Railway Applications e.g. ETCS (§5.1.5); RMR SPECTRUM per EC Decision 2021/1730: 874.4–880.0 / 919.4–925.0 MHz paired + 1900–1910 MHz unpaired, 3GPP 5G NR (M), operator implements 900 or 1900 or both (§8.4); BEARER FLEXIBILITY (§12): FRMCS Multipath across FRMCS AND NON-FRMCS transport domains — public MNOs, non-terrestrial/satellite, WLAN, 4G EPS — with transport-domain selection/transition TRANSPARENT to the application stratum (M), infrastructure-configurable rule set (path quality × session attributes), aggregation (O), standards-based (IETF) (M); Multi Access ATSSS-based (M); Intra-RAT incl. RAN SHARING with a public MNO (M); QoS (§14): E2E KPIs at the app↔FRMCS reference point — immediate session establishment ≤1 s / normal ≤3 s (99%-ile), talker assignment <300 ms (99%-ile emergency / 95%-ile other), standardized 5QI set {5,7,65,67,69,76}, ARP 1–8 (M), MC-server-driven QoS signalling into the 5GC PCF (M), predefined defaults when unidentified (M); CYBERSECURITY (§15): defence-in-depth, service/transport strata security functionally independent, GDPR functions (M); MINIMUM SECURITY LEVEL "implemented and applied" (M, Table 8): AEAD_AES_128_GCM (MCX media/signalling, TS 33.180), OpenID Connect 1.0 MCX user auth, 128-NEA1/128-NIA1, EAP-AKA′ + 5G AKA (TS 33.501). WHAT v1.0.0 EXPLICITLY DOES NOT SPECIFY (editor's notes on the face — the gap map): §9 GSM-R INTERWORKING & MIGRATION — EMPTY, deferred to a later version (→ ETSI TR 103 768 / TS 103 792); §10 interconnection/roaming/border-crossing — not addressed; §13 NETWORK SLICING — not addressed; §16 positioning — not addressed; §17 non-functional reqs incl. §17.2 FRMCS SYSTEM MONITORING — header-only, not addressed; real-time QoS monitoring/logging of individual sessions FFS (§14.7.10); §15.2.1 places patching, logging, MONITORING, anomaly detection, incident response OUT OF SRS SCOPE (→ complementary security guidelines) and risk assessments are not covered; §15.3 FFS: multipath/multi-access security, non-terrestrial, FALL BACK TO PMNO AND GSM-R; Table 6 Note 3: experts recommend AEAD_AES_256_GCM for MCDATA as AES-128 "will become vulnerable within the foreseeable lifespan of FRMCS". READ: pp. 1–20, 45–49, 65–88 of 109 (cover/history/ToC, spectrum+interworking, bearer flexibility/QoS/security/monitoring); §7 on-board detail, §11 identifiers, §18–22 + annexes not read this pass | Normative sector specification (UIC official publication, FRMCS standards track) | "index094_-_FRMCS_SRS_(AT-7800)_v100.pdf", Download folder (provenance URL not recorded — capture before public citation) | A (primary; VINTAGE FLAG: v1.0.0 Feb 2023 baseline since evolved — V2 presented 2024, V3 pilots edition due Nov 2026 per E-2026-07-11-01 — currency-check (M)-clauses against the live edition before load-bearing use; © warning explicit → paraphrase only) | R4, R6, R7 (+ R2 §9 gap, R5 KPI/slicing, R12 §17.2 monitoring gap, R14 §15 scope split; PR15/ADR-007(e)) | validate | A: primary normative UIC publication with controlled document history and working-group governance — none of the B/C/D patterns; vintage flag as stated. SIX HOOKS: (1) R6 — the gateway-decoupling decision now has its FRMCS-side normative anchor (§5.1.4/§12.1.1: transport evolves, applications untouched) to pair with the TOBA thread; (2) R4 — DB's post-incident "fallback level via public mobile radio" (E-2026-06-27-03) is STANDARDS-CONFORMANT BY DESIGN: multipath over public-MNO/satellite/WLAN domains is a specified (M) capability with rule-set governance — but the solution choice is FFS (§12.7.4) and fallback SECURITY is FFS (§15.3), so the direction is anchored while the design is unfinished at this baseline (multipath ≠ reliability caveat E-2026-07-06-10 and flap-trigger lesson E-2026-07-12-01 both still apply — the rule set §12.4.10-14 is exactly where independent-health/hysteresis logic must live); (3) R7 — spectrum M-clauses tie interoperability directly to EC Decision 2021/1730 (900/1900 MHz, 5G NR); (4) THE SHARP FINDING, R12/ADR-002: at this baseline the bearer spec gives the oversight layer NO STANDARDISED TELEMETRY SURFACE — §17.2 system monitoring empty, per-session QoS monitoring/notification FFS (§14.7.10, incl. notifying the application when QoS is not satisfied), monitoring/anomaly-detection/incident-response excluded from SRS scope (§15.2.1) → the autonomous-oversight architecture must BRING ITS OWN observability (out-of-band monitoring thread E-2026-06-30-03 kin); the 23-Jun silent-fault lesson is not answered by the bearer standard at v1.0.0; (5) R2 — §9 interworking EMPTY in Feb 2023: primary-source corroboration of the spec-gap chronology behind "EU spec unfinalised → no procurement yet" (E-2026-06-27-03, E-2026-07-11-01); NB R5's mechanism line cites SLICING, unaddressed at this baseline (§13) — check V2/V3 fill-in before citing slicing as the QoS mechanism; (6) R14/ADR-012 — CRA secure-by-design + vulnerability-handling duties land largely in the space §15.2.1 excludes from the SRS (patching, logging, incident response) → CRA conformance cannot lean on SRS compliance alone; AES-128-lifespan note = crypto-agility flag for procurement (R8 kin). WATCH hooks: V2/V2.2/V3 fill-in of §9/§13/§16/§17 (V3 Nov 2026 = the pilots edition, E-2026-07-11-01). No decision moved; no matrix/ADR status change (validates mechanisms already Accepted/Open) |

| E-2026-07-12-04 | 2026-07-12 | Master's thesis (Tesi di Laurea Magistrale in Ingegneria Informatica), Univ. Napoli Federico II, AY 2020/2021 — Francesco Vitale, supervisors Prof. Nicola Mazzocca + Prof. Francesco Flammini: "Run-time Anomaly Detection with Process Mining: Methodology and Railway System Compliance Case-Study", 146+ pp. Paraphrased: a Process-Mining CONFORMANCE-CHECKING methodology (token replay) that validates the RUN-TIME behaviour of an ERTMS/ETCS system against DESIGN-TIME process models (BPMN → Petri nets) extracted from the ETCS SRS — case study: START OF MISSION, Level 2 Full Supervision. A Fitness metric with PER-COMPONENT weights (λ_DMI/λ_EVC/λ_RTM/λ_RBC) both detects anomalies and classifies their SEVERITY and PHYSICAL LOCALIZATION: perfect runs fitness 1.00; DETECTED errors (system rolls forward, e.g. RBC unavailable + 2 retries) 0.93–0.97, severity Very Low; LATENT errors (corrupted RBC/EVC phases) 0.34–0.77, severity Medium — the metric SEPARATES DETECTED FROM LATENT FAULTS from the event trace alone. Fault-injection campaign on a distributed lab prototype (Raspberry Pis + laptop, MQTT pub/sub; DMI/EVC/OB/RTM/RBC components; code on GitHub): RBC unavailability, sensor failure, RBC-connection loss mid-routine, phase corruption; fault taxonomy explicitly includes INTRUSION ATTEMPTS (compromised node → "harder-to-detect, possibly latent" anomalies) vs easily-detected physical interference. Classification results (synthetic-augmented, leave-one-out): severity J48 84% (best config C2, 100 instances); localization MLP 77.3% (66 instances). Architecture note: OB↔RBC assumed on GSM-R "or possibly in the future FRMCS" [sic "FRCMS"]; the EXTERNAL anomaly-detector link deliberately on a SEPARATE wireless path (LoRaWAN/Sigfox suggested). | E-2026-07-24-01 | 2026-07-24 | ACM CCS '25 (Shaik, Jaschek, Seifert — TU Berlin): "Uncovering hidden paths in 5G core: Exploiting protocol tunneling and network boundary bridging". Empirical security evaluation of six production-grade 5G SA cores (open-source Open5GS, free5GC, OAI-5G, SD-Core; two commercial PC1/PC2) using Tunneler, a Scapy-based UE-side probing tool. Paraphrased: a compromised UE (commercial smartphone + valid SIM) abuses two MITRE-FiGHT vectors — PROTOCOL TUNNELING (GTP-U-in-GTP-U: the UPF treats the inner GTP header as opaque payload, performs no nested-header inspection, and mis-routes to an attacker-chosen inner TEID/IP) and NETWORK BOUNDARY BRIDGING (user-plane traffic reaches control-plane NFs — AMF via NGAP injection, SMF/UPF via PFCP injection — because N2/N4 IPsec is "often disabled in deployments that assume physical security" and UPFs lack egress/routing enforcement). 7 vulnerabilities + 6 undocumented weaknesses across misrouting logic, insufficient validation, and spec ambiguity. Identifier weaknesses: 32-bit TEID often uses only low 16 bits + sequential allocation → enumerable <11 s at 1000 pps; Open5GS SEID uses only 12 bits (space 4096, scanned <9 s); NO core rate-limited error responses. Three validated attacks: A1 direct UE-to-UE data injection (bypasses the external data network + network-layer defences); A2 charging fraud via reflection (spoofed victim uplink TEID; amplified DNS reflection consumed ~46 KB on the victim with no attacker downlink billed); A3 full traffic interception / Person-in-the-Middle via a rogue gNodeB registered through NGAP boundary-bridging — "existing 5G security mechanisms [mutual auth, encryption, integrity, downgrade prevention] do not defend against PITM". DoS: SD-Core crashed on malformed PFCP (manual restart); Open5GS/free5GC terminated all sessions under PFCP session-table saturation → forced re-registration. Mitigations proposed: bind SEID to source identifier (as TEID-IP already is), mandate full identifier entropy, specify PFCP handling of empty/malformed messages, cap GTP-U encapsulation at one layer (inspect first 64 bytes), rate-limit control-plane messages + per-IP PFCP session quotas, deny-list N3→N4/internal re-entry, IPsec on N2/N4. Responsibly disclosed to core maintainers + industry bodies; all tests lab-isolated (Faraday cage) | Academic research (peer-reviewed — ACM CCS, top-tier security venue) | Altaf Shaik, Robert Jaschek, Jean-Pierre Seifert (TU Berlin); ACM CCS '25, Taipei, Oct 2025; file "Uncovering_hidden_paths_in_5G_core...pdf", Download folder | A | R14 (+ R5 target-core surface; R3/R4 DoS/availability) | validate | A: peer-reviewed primary empirical research at a top-tier venue, named academic authors, first-hand evaluation of six real 5G cores — strongest evidence class in the log for the FRMCS-target attack surface, above the unrefereed Vitale thesis (E-2026-07-12-04) on rigour. TIER CAVEATS: (1) CAMERA-READY/preprint — DOI (10.1145/nnnnnnn.nnnnnnn) + ACM ISBN are PLACEHOLDERS, no final page numbers → capture the final ACM DL version before load-bearing external citation; (2) 5G-GENERIC, NOT RAIL-SPECIFIC — six open-source/anonymised-commercial cores, none FRMCS/rail-procured; applicability to FRMCS is by INHERITANCE (FRMCS = 3GPP 5G SA, same UPF/GTP-U/NGAP/PFCP surface), an inference not a rail test; (3) lab-isolated + responsibly disclosed — some cores may already be patched. WHY VALIDATE (not revise): strengthens R14's secure-by-design threat basis with hard primary evidence; does NOT flip a status — R14 stays Open, ADR-012 stays Proposed. SUBSTANTIATES the CSO-validation (cso-validation-2026-07-21.md): §1 "5G SBA API exploitation, slice mis-isolation" now backed by empirical UPF/NGAP/PFCP exploitation; §3 zone/conduit — NBB is EMPIRICAL PROOF the control/user-plane boundary (candidate zone Z2 FRMCS core) is bridgeable under weak routing enforcement, a conduit-boundary failure not a theoretical one; PR19 candidate (DoS on FRMCS SBA surfaces) — SD-Core crash + session-table saturation are concrete instances (R3/R4 bearing); A3 PITM defeating mutual-auth/encryption is the security analogue of the silent-fault lesson (standard 5G controls do not self-report this compromise). ENRICHES ADR-012 action-2 (this row): the procurement clause now names the paper's testable 5G-core controls — N2/N4 IPsec mandatory, UPF egress/routing enforcement, GTP-U single-encapsulation-depth limit, full TEID/SEID entropy, PFCP source-binding + malformed-message handling, control-plane rate-limiting — which also become ADR-007-style Tunneler-class test surfaces for any FRMCS 5GC bid. Cross-refs: E-2026-07-12-02 (SRS v1.0.0 §15.3 slicing FFS + §17.2 monitoring empty — this paper is the threat behind those gaps); E-2026-07-20-01 (ENISA 5G SBA/slicing exposure kin). ADR-012 action-2 enriched (this row); NO status flip — R14 stays Open, ADR-012 stays Proposed, matrix status column unchanged |

ADDS NOTHING: every factual claim is already held A-tier primary via DB (E-2026-06-27-01/-03) — software fault on a network-distribution/switch component during maintenance → no error notification → automatic failover to the functional redundancy never engaged → manual recovery → nationwide safe-stop → cyberattack ruled out. QUALITY FLAGS: (1) propagates the "~90 minutes" recovery figure the engagement EXPLICITLY REJECTS (paper-2 binding constraint: ~2 hours, first trains ≈00:30; never "90 minutes") — the article's "90 minutes" + "00:50 restoration" + "22:30 start" timestamps are UNVERIFIED against the DB-confirmed record and must not be adopted; (2) states "backup did not automatically activate" as reconstruction, whereas the record treats the failover mechanism as design-INFERENCE until DB publishes telemetry (PR8). NEVER CITE. DOCUMENTARY VALUE ONLY: an instance of the outage narrative entering third-party consulting thought-leadership (narrative-spread signal); tangentially an AI-consultancy touching the exact incident this engagement analyses, but the article makes NO AI-oversight argument (no bearing on R9/R12). No decision moved; no matrix/ADR change; R3/R4/R12 status unaffected |
